# Proposed OEIS comments: A205920 to A207406

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A205920

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A205920>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [5292](../papers/5001-5500/5292-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) +37*a(n-2) -134*a(n-3) -468*a(n-4) +446*a(n-5) +3506*a(n-6) +2929*a(n-7) +764*a(n-8) -27570*a(n-9) -19322*a(n-10) -4621*a(n-11) +18764*a(n-12) +42723*a(n-13) +11896*a(n-14) -35641*a(n-15) -21691*a(n-16) +23786*a(n-17) +548*a(n-18) -9887*a(n-19) +7551*a(n-20) -3387*a(n-21) +1201*a(n-22) -314*a(n-23) +71*a(n-24) -13*a(n-25) +a(n-26)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 64 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 26. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A206163

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A206163>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2673](../papers/2501-3000/2673-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) + 217*a(n-2) - 33*a(n-3) - 1320*a(n-4) - 432*a(n-5) + 1152*a(n-6) for n>7.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(85 + 985*x - 2373*x^2 - 8345*x^3 + 3536*x^4 + 10032*x^5 - 2688*x^6) / ((1 - x - 4*x^2)*(1 - 213*x^2 - 180*x^3 + 288*x^4)). - _Colin Barker_, Jun 13 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix: the subblock condition looks only at a bounded window of consecutive lines, and "new values introduced in row major order" -- which is not a condition on any window, since it counts colourings up to renaming -- enters only through how many values have been introduced so far, one extra integer in the state. That gives 14 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

That gives 76 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 32. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026

## A206377

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A206377>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2857](../papers/2501-3000/2857-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 36*a(n-1) - 377*a(n-2) + 1398*a(n-3) - 2064*a(n-4) + 1008*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(85 - 1691*x + 8706*x^2 - 16640*x^3 + 10320*x^4) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x)*(1 - 21*x)*(1 - 12*x + 24*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Jun 15 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix: the subblock condition looks only at a bounded window of consecutive lines, and "new values introduced in row major order" -- which is not a condition on any window, since it counts colourings up to renaming -- enters only through how many values have been introduced so far, one extra integer in the state. That gives 12 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A206839

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A206839>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3060](../papers/3001-3500/3060-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 21 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A207020

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207020>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2983](../papers/2501-3000/2983-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 6*n^3 + (27/2)*n^2 - (21/2)*n.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jun 17 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 91 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 150 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A207106

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207106>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3029](../papers/3001-3500/3029-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (4/3)*n^3 + 8*n^2 - (10/3)*n.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 20 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 43 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A207165

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207165>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2984](../papers/2501-3000/2984-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 9*n^3 + 9*n - 9.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jun 19 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 91 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [4710](../papers/4501-5000/4710-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) -6*a(n-2) +24*a(n-3) -87*a(n-4) +24*a(n-5) -155*a(n-6) +534*a(n-7) +401*a(n-8) +731*a(n-9) -1499*a(n-10) -2615*a(n-11) -3148*a(n-12) +1796*a(n-13) +6170*a(n-14) +7794*a(n-15) +630*a(n-16) -7609*a(n-17) -10518*a(n-18) -4095*a(n-19) +4874*a(n-20) +8369*a(n-21) +4506*a(n-22) -1299*a(n-23) -3802*a(n-24) -2467*a(n-25) -86*a(n-26) +905*a(n-27) +680*a(n-28) +144*a(n-29) -96*a(n-30) -82*a(n-31) -27*a(n-32) -a(n-33) +4*a(n-34) +2*a(n-35)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 81 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 35. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A207255

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207255>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2765](../papers/2501-3000/2765-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (5/6)*n^4 + (35/3)*n^3 - (5/6)*n^2 - (5/3)*n.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jun 21 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical closed form is correct. The count is a walk count in a finite graph, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; the conjectured form satisfies the same one, and the two agree at enough consecutive indices to agree from then on, for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 144 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 24. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
